研究員/王建智 MICHAELWANG82121188@UNI-
PSG.COM

研究部對於長榮看法轉趨正向，係因：(1) 3Q26 受惠貨櫃航運
旺季及運價回升，平均運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅， 帶動獲
利顯著成長。(2) 4Q26 雖進入傳統淡季，惟北美農曆春節前備
貨 、歐洲貨量成長及亞洲區域航線旺季提供支撐，預期獲利雖低
於 3Q26，但仍優於 2Q26。(3) 2027 年雖面臨新船交付增加的供
給壓力， 但全球碼頭擴建速度落後船隊成長，加上塞港及紅海繞
航持續消耗有效運力，有助減緩新增供給對運價的衝擊， 北美及
歐洲航線長約運價亦預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利。預估
2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 分別為45.21 元（+42.7%YoY）／27.89 元
（-38.3%YoY）。歷史本淨比區間為 0.4～1.8 倍，以 2027 年底
預估每股淨值 298.36 元為評價基礎，給予0.95 倍本淨比， 目標
價 283 元，將投資評等調升至買進。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 運價漲幅高於燃油成本增幅，帶動獲利顯著成長 
長榮 3Q26 營收 1,467.76 億元，+39.6%QoQ/+51.4%YoY，主要受
惠第三季貨櫃航運傳統旺季， 歐美航線進入零售商備貨高峰， 帶
動貨運需求回升，加上紅海繞航、 全球港口壅塞持續消耗有效運
力，以及航商透過艙位控管調節供給，推升主要航線運價。7、8
月平均運費分別為 1,539 及1,577 美元／TEU， 參考9 月SCFI 月
均 3,657 點，+8.0%MoM， 推估9 月平均運費約 1,703 美元／TEU，
並假設 9 月貨量與 8 月持平，則 3Q26 平均運費約 1,606 美元／
TEU，+44.7%QoQ，貨量276 萬 TEU，-1.8%QoQ，營收成長主要由
運價上漲貢獻。

成本方面 ， 預估每噸耗油成本由2Q26 的 645 美元升至 714 美元 ，
+10.6%QoQ，假設季度耗油量持平，燃油費用增加約22 億元，惟
運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅 ， 營收增加帶來的獲利貢獻可望
大幅超過成本上升影響。預估 3Q26 稅後淨利 479.69 億元，
+199.2%QoQ/+120.6%YoY，稅後EPS 22.16 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

圖一、各月平均運費、每噸耗油成本、貨量

資料來源：長榮，統一投顧整理 
2. 4Q26 進入傳統淡季，惟區域航線需求支撐營運 
第四季為貨櫃航運傳統淡季， 整體需求不如第三季旺季強勁， 惟
根據訂艙及裝載情況 ， 至11 月前貨量仍具支撐 。 北美航線方面 ，
美國主要港口進口貨櫃量維持高檔，零售庫存銷售比低於 1.3，
顯示零售商庫存水位相對偏低，仍有持續補貨需求，預期 11 月
貨量隨淡季趨緩 ，12 月則有農曆春節前備貨需求支撐 。 歐洲航線
方面 ，2026 年貨量成長優於預期 ， 雖運價自7 月後逐步回落 ， 但
紅海地緣政治風險尚未解除， 繞航仍持續消耗有效運力。 亞洲區
域航線第四季進入傳統旺季 ， 受惠中國加一帶動東南亞及南亞原
料、半成品運輸需求成長。此外，全球塞港造成約300 萬至 400
萬 TEU 有效運力損失，占全球總運力約 11%至 12%，搭配航商艙
位控管，有助支撐淡季運價。

預估 4Q26 營收 1,174.21 億元，-20.0%QoQ/+37.0%YoY，稅後淨
利 255.68 億元，-46.7%QoQ/+200.2%YoY，稅後 EPS 11.81 元。

3. 2027 年新船交付壓力增加，港口壅塞限制有效運力供給 
2027~2029 年為全球貨櫃船新船交付高峰，目前全球貨櫃船總運
力約3,400 萬TEU，至2030 年待交付新船運力超過1,400 萬TEU，
主流研調機構(Clarksons、Drewry)預估 2027 年名目運力供給增
速為 7.7~7.9%，供給成長壓力仍是 2027 年主要風險。惟名目運
力增加不代表有效運力等幅成長 ， 過去十年全球碼頭處理能力年
均成長約 2.7%，低於船隊運力年均成長約 5.4%，港口基礎設施
擴張速度落後船隊成長，塞港問題短期難以改善。紅海及蘇伊士
運河恢復通行亦為重要變數， 歐洲航線若恢復通行，單程約節省

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

7 天 、 往返約14 天 ， 但船舶集中抵港可能加劇碼頭壅塞 ， 抵銷部
分航程縮短釋出的運力。長榮截至 2030 年尚有 72 艘新船待交
付 ， 合計運力約89 萬 TEU， 並持續投資重要樞紐碼頭 ， 提升靠泊
效率及船隊調度彈性。

4. 2027 年歐美長約運價預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利 
2027 年北美及歐洲航線長約運價預期均高於 2026 年，主要受惠
三項因素 。 首先 ，2026 年第三季貨櫃運價大幅上漲 ， 目前現貨運
價明顯高於前一年度長約議價時的水準 ， 為新年度合約價格調升
奠定基礎 。 其次 ， 全球港口處理能力擴張速度落後船隊運力成長 ，
加上紅海繞航及主要港口壅塞持續消耗有效運力 ， 航商供給調節
能力提升，有利維持長約議價能力。最後，歐美航線貨運需求仍
具支撐 ， 北美零售商庫存水位偏低 ， 歐洲航線貨量成長優於預期 ，
提供長約價格上調條件。歐洲航線新約自 2027 年初開始適用，
北美航線則自 5 月起生效 ， 預期新年度長約運價調升將逐步反映
於營收，並有助減緩現貨運價波動對獲利的影響，支撐 2027 年
營運表現。

5. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 45.21/27.89 元，塞港及長約運
價調升支撐獲利高於歷史平均水準 
預估 2026/2027 年營收分別為 4,558.69／4,178.70 億元，分別
+20.3%YoY/-8.3%YoY，稅後淨利分別為 978.74／603.81 億元，
分別+42.7%YoY/-38.3%YoY，稅後 EPS 分別為 45.21 元／27.89
元。2026 年受惠第三季運價大幅上漲 ， 帶動全年獲利回升 。2027
年雖面臨新船交付增加及運價自高檔回落的壓力 ， 惟全球碼頭處
理能力成長落後船隊運力，加上塞港及紅海繞航限制有效供給，
歐美航線長約價格亦預期高於 2026 年 ， 有助減緩獲利下滑幅度 。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

附 錄 二 ： 免 責 宣 言

告 中 所 有 的 意 見 及 預 估 ， 如 有 變 更 恕 不 另 行 通 知 。

本 研 究 報 告 所 載 之 投 資 資 訊 ， 僅 提 供 客 戶 做 為 一 般 投 資 參 考 ， 並 非 針 對 特 定 對 象 提
供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資
料 來 源 ， 但 概 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 作 出 任 何
陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非
（且不應 解 釋 為 ）在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 非 依 法 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 人 士 或
公 司 ， 為 於 該 管 轄 區 內 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 遊 說 。 本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 統 一 投 顧 之
著 作 權 ， 嚴 禁 抄 襲 與 仿 造 。

## Document 104: 261012_ml_TSMC

### Page 1

Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co.

3Q26 earnings preview: steeper 2nm ramp 
lifting sales upside at resilient margins

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 3,260 TWD | Price: 2,550 TWD

Solid HPC and flagship mobile pipeline solidifies 2H26 
TSMC’s 3Q26 sales were above street +18% QoQ in NT$ and reached US$46.7bn (vs. its 
guide of US$44.6-45.8bn) which we believe should be driven by the builds for Apple’s 
mobile SoC and AMD’s server CPU on 2nm along with multiple GPU and ASIC product 
refreshes migrating to 3nm. The mix upgrades could lift 4Q26 sales by +10-15% QoQ, 
putting it on track to the guidance for 40%+ YoY in ’26. While utilization and pricing are 
favorable, GMs could hold steady in 3Q/4Q26 (BofAe 67.0%/67.8%) rather than expand 
(recent street 67-68%/68-69%) factoring dilution from 2nm and overseas fab ramps. 
Tight supply enables greater value capture and investment 
On solid AI demand outlook, TSMC’s capex should be on track to its lifted guidance of 
US$60-64bn in ’26 (BofAe US$63bn). The investment could see another surge in ’27 
(BofAe US$83bn) for further N3, N2, A16 and CoWoS-L expansion and new technologies 
ramp (e.g. CoPoS and CPO). The tool makers’ modest price hike could require TSMC to 
spend 10% more capex though should be reflected in its value when the capacity starts 
production in ’28 on top of a 5-10% leading edge ASP lift in 1Q27 on supply tightness. 
Checkpoints: AI demand, capex & competition/partnership 
1) TSMC’s view on cloud AI investment amid rate hike + challenging FCF, 2) comment on 
the market and its growth and capex commitment, 3) update on the competition and 
collaboration (see Exhibit 29 for our analysis on Terafab/SpaceX) and US investment.  
AI-led earnings outlook firmer; valuation should catch up 
We lift our 26/27/28 EPS by 2-9% to NT$112/NT$161/NT$203 and keep our Buy rating 
on TSMC and raise PO to NT$3,260/US$620 on 20x ‘27E P/E. Its current valuation is 
undemanding at 16x ‘27E P/E (vs. its long term 11-21x) backed by its advanced 
technology leadership.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
TSMC was founded in 1987 and is listed in Taiwan 
(2330TT) and the US (TSM/NYS).TSMC is the largest and 
global leader in integrated circuit (IC) manufacturing. As a 
build-to-order foundry, it provides a wide range of value-
add activities: IC manufacturing, mask-making, IC design 
services, turnkey solutions, and process development. We 
attribute its success to its proven, winning business model, 
unparalleled scale advantage, optimized execution, and 
technology scope and depth. 5 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
The ongoing semi content growth in mobile, rise of artificial 
intelligence (AI), and proliferation of Internet of Things (IoT) 
should result in sustainable upside in aggregate computing 
power globally. TSMC, as the leading contract manufacturer 
of semiconductor chips, is in a good position to capitalize. 
We are structurally positive on TSMC and expect it to 
sustain over 15% growth with rising FCF from 2026 
onwards.

### Page 4

3Q26 sales topped guidance; GMs tracking
well but recent street could be too bullish
We profile our expectation for TSMC’s 3Q26 earnings on October 15th. 3Q26 sales were
reported +18% QoQ in NT$, above BofAe/street expectations of +14%/+13% QoQ.
Based on the average FX of NT$31.99/US$ (vs. its NT$32US$ guide), its sales in US$
term would be US$46.7bn, also above its guidance range of US$44.6-45.8bn. we believe
the strength is led by strong ramp on 2nm potentially growing to 11% of its sales in

3Q26 (vs. 3% in 2Q26) for Apple’s builds for its product launches and surging demand
for AMD’s Venice server CPU. These growth drivers on the node are supplemented by
the incremental inventory stocking from Mediatek and Qualcomm for their flagship
smartphone SoCs albeit at a low base and below seasonal due to weak Android demand.
On top of the 2nm scale up, we expect the utilization for 5nm and 3nm to stay above
100% backed by the continued HPC/AI build strength especially with multiple product

upgrades including 1) accelerators (GPUs and ASICs): strong demand for Nvidia’s Rubin
GPU, AMD’s MI400, AWS’s Trainium3 ramp and initial ramp for Google’s TPUv8t/i more
than offsetting a mild wind down in Google’s Ironwood TPU, 2) CPUs: solid demand for
general purpose servers and AI data centers as noted in our deep dive report quantifying
CPU demand through ’30 across x86 (AMD’s Turin CPUs) and ARM based CPUs (e.g.
Nvidia’s Vera CPU, Google’s Axion CPU, AWS Graviton CPU along with incremental

contribution from Microsoft Cobalt CPU) and 3) networking switch: more and faster
data transmission in the complex AI data centers requires more advanced  networking
switch solutions from Nvidia/Mellanox, Broadcom and Marvell.
The company’s better than expected sales in 3Q26 backed by healthy shipment and
pricing trend on the advanced nodes should be supportive to its GMs. However, in
contrast to the recent aggressive street expectations of 67-68% GMs in 3Q26, we only

model it at the high-end of its guidance of 65-67%, balancing more favorable utilization
and gradual 3nm depreciation roll off partially offset by a 3-4% GMs dilution from 2nm
ramp which we believe is driver for the incremental sales during the quarter.

### Page 5

HPC/Apple lifting 4Q26 sales outlook;
resilient GM expectation but below street
For 4Q26, based on its slightly above +40% YoY full year sales guide, TSMC’s sales could
grow 10-15% QoQ (BofAe +11%), better than its 10-year seasonality of 7% QoQ even
off a high base from 1Q-3Q26. The blended ASPs strength (BofAe +11% QoQ) should be
the key driver for 4Q26 sales as shipment mix continues to shift toward advanced nodes,
with revenue from 5nm and below processes representing 75% of the company’s sales

(vs. 66%/71% in 2Q/3Q26). Specifically, 2nm with 15%+ higher wafer pricing than 3nm
could further grow its contribution to 18% of TSMC’s sales during the quarter. On the
other hand, we model a stable wafer shipment (BofAe +9%/-5% QoQ for
advanced/mature nodes), balancing HPC demand strength and Apple’s seasonal chipset
builds for its new product launches and a modest drop on the specialty process output
and effective capacity loss due to equipment movement for the node conversion.

While the blended pricing is even more favorable and utilization on the advanced and
mature nodes in general stay healthy, we model a relatively stable GMs only up to
~67.8% levels in 4Q26 (vs. recent street and investors’ expectations at 68-69%). The key
factors we consider as mildly negative offset would be 1) higher depreciation: +25-30%
HoH in 2H26 due to more overseas and 2nm capacity ramp and 2) 2nm still going
through yield learning curve and could see more meaningful impact in 4Q26 (vs. its 3-4%

GMs dilution guide in 2H26) as the sales contribution grows.

### Page 6

2026-27 sales and margins outlook 
solidified by healthy AI demand 
Factoring in stronger CPU and AI GPU/ASIC demand outlook as noted in our Semicon 
Taiwan takeaways report where we updated our industry models, we are lifting our US$ 
sales expectation for TSMC in ‘26/’27 to +42%/+40% YoY respectively (vs. BofA prior 
+41%/+40% YoY). More importantly, with utilization staying tighter for longer along with 
inflationary cost pass through (e.g. equipment and consumables), TSMC should raise its 
advanced node and packaging pricing by 5-10% in the beginning of 2027 to reflect its 
value. This along with a mild 3-5% price hike on the mature nodes where the utilization 
is back to 90-95% as it optimizes the product mix following the strategic 6”/8” capacity 
retirement should lift its GMs to 67%/69% in 2026/27 (vs. BofA prior 67%/67%).

Capex on track for lifted guide for 2026 
and will grow 20%+ CAGR from ’26-28 
We lifted our front-end foundry industry capex growth expectation to 19%/26% YoY for 
‘26/27 to US$86bn/US$108bn, supporting a CAGR of 16% from ’26-28. This is mostly 
led by our higher expectation on TSMC’s capex on stronger wafer demand on N3 and N2 
from GPUs, ASICs, CPUs, networking switches and HBM base dies and initial ramp of 
A16, along with incremental spending reacceleration from tier 2 foundries. 
Separately, we also raised the back-end packaging and testing industry spending 
estimates from 66%/36% YoY to 82%/21% YoY for ‘26/27 to US$27bn/US$33bn, 
supporting a CAGR of 15% from ’26-28. The upward revision is driven by our higher 
expectation of TSMC’s more aggressive capacity addition on CoWoS-R/L to 
accommodate more demand from its customers amid growing level of chipset 
complexity and reticle size and higher level of capacity readiness of SoIC for higher 3D 
packaging adoption for logic and memory dies integration and CPO. The more 
aggressive spending from ASE is set to grow by another 30-50% YoY in 2027 off a high 
base in 2026 on its own FoCoS capacity ramp and TSMC’s CoWoS licensing.

Exhibit 12: Back-end sales, capex, and capital intensity

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Specifically on TSMC, based on our bottom up capacity analysis across front-end 
manufacturing and back-end packaging, TSMC’s capex spending is tracking to US$63bn 
for 2026 (vs. its latest guidance of US$60-64bn) mostly skewed toward advanced front-
end manufacturing including 55-60k WPM 3nm capacity addition, 60k WPM 2nm 
capacity ramp, initial 3-5k WPM A16 capacity installation. The back-end packaging 
investment should be largely driven by its CoWoS capacity growth while the spending on 
the equipment pull-in for CPO and CoPoS remains limited as most of the equipment are 
still under sampling process for qualification and meaningful shipment will be in 2027-
28. 
In addition to the earlier tool pull-in schedule due to tighter equipment supply with 
memory makers expanding, the fast-growing capex should reflect the rising cost per 1k 
WPM on the advanced nodes vs. mature nodes, with the equipment cost per 1k WPM 
advanced wafer capacity expansion has grown from US$156mn in 2023 to US$241mn in 
2025. Even factoring in more common use of equipment on the advanced nodes, the 
capex/1k WPM advanced capacity could further rise by 20% to US$289mn in 2027 with 
the technology architecture upgrade. 
On top of the investment in the core front-end manufacturing, we expect the company 
to slightly outspend in the back-end packaging and estimate it is at the upper half of its 
guidance for 10-20% total capex, mainly for more aggressive capacity expansion to 
meet its customers’ demand amid growing competition from Intel’s EMIB-T solution and 
technology innovation. On the other hand, the maintenance capex could be reduced for 
specialty technologies as the company gradually retires the equipment.

its sales of +32% CAGR, sustaining GMs expansion to 68-69% levels by 2028.

Capacity expansion accelerates across 
advanced front-end and back-end 
As TSMC is in the process to retire a portion of its equipment on 6”, 8” and mature 12” 
processes while still adding the capacity on the advanced nodes, we estimate the 
company’s total front-end manufacturing capacity will drop by 2% YoY in 2026. 
Exhibit 17: TSMC's year-end capacity by node for 20nm and below 
TSMC will expand aggressively on 3nm and 2nm through 2027

Source: Company Data, BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Exhibit 18: TSMC's advanced node capacity as % of its installed base 
TSMC's advanced nodes will reach 40% of its total capacity

Source: Company Data, BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

The trend could be more stable into 2027-28 as TSMC strives to optimize its capacity 
mix and across node conversion to accommodate its customers’ demand especially on 
the leading-edge technologies, with its advanced capacity set to grow by 10% CAGR 
from 2025-28 balanced by the mature capacity reduction at -8% CAGR. 
Based on our estimate, TSMC’s capacity for 7nm and below nodes will grow from 320k 
WPM per month in 2023 to ~700k WPM by 2028 at a 16% CAGR, through expansion and 
efficient conversion from higher equipment commonality, cross technology planning and 
AI productivity gains (e.g. tool idle time and chamber cleaning schedule optimization). 
This will also support the mix of 7nm and below capacity climb toward 40-45% of its 
total capacity (vs. 24% in 2023). By region, we expect the capacity expansion through 
2027 to enable its overseas fabs to account for 17% of TSMC’s total capacity by 2028 
(vs. 7% in 2020) and similarly for the advanced node capacity (vs. ~0% in 2023), mainly 
in Arizona and Kumamoto. 
Specifically, we believe the company is on track to its target on 25% CAGR capacity 
expansion for its 5nm and 3nm from ’22-27. We model its 3nm capacity will reach 
190k/230k WPM by 4Q26/27. On N2 family, TSMC plans to grow capacity by 70% CAGR 
from ’26-28, with 1st year ramp delivering 45% more capacity vs. 3nm as it rolls out 5 
fabs at once.

On the back-end packaging, with surging server CPU demand on top of the already 
strong order backlog for GPUs and ASICs, we estimate the industry’s CoWoS supply will 
need to grow by 52% CAGR from 2026-28. TSMC will still lead the capacity expansion 
from 120k WPM in 4Q26 to 210k WPM in 4Q27, while the OSATs will also grow their

in 4Q26) and 25-30k WPM (vs. 15-20k by 4Q26) in 4Q27 respectively. Specifically, while 
TSMC should still lead the supply for complex GPU and ASIC packaging, ASE grows as a 
major 2nd source with its capacity share growing from 18% of the industry in ’26 to 29% 
in ’28 and proven track record as the major CoWoS full process service provider for 
AMD’s Venice server CPU.

### Page 14

Solid technology roadmap and execution 
easing competition concern 
TSMC is pursuing a dual track strategy for its front-end manufacturing, advancing the 
solution for both its consumer and HPC customers. The nodes targeted for consumer 
chipsets requiring cost efficiency and IP reuse will be introduced on an annual basis to 
match its customers’ yearly product launch schedule. For the HPC applications, the 
company targets to roll out a new node with biennial cadence, supporting major leaps in 
performance, transistor density, and power density. 
Exhibit 27: Front-end manufacturing roadmap comparison 
TSMC's advanced node technology roadmap advancing faster vs. Intel and Samsung and has the widest offerings targeted for different applications

Source: BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
This is evidenced in its N2 family and below nodes, with N2U ramping in ’28 for mobile 
applications and A13 node in ’29 which will be backward compatible to A14. It will also 
have A12 node in ’29 integrated SPR to address power integrity and current delivery 
constraint suitable for AI/HPC systems. The strategy along with the continued expansion 
of the derivative offerings for main nodes should position TSMC well to defend its 
market share with its competitors claiming their progress on the advanced 
manufacturing. 
Specifically, Intel’s 18A has been in mass production since 4Q25 for its Panther Lake 
and Clearwater Forest chipsets though yield will not be fully fine tuned until 1Q27 at 25-
30k WPM capacity scale in its Oregon and Arizona fabs. The follow on derivative 
technology, 18A-P, has recently started risk production featuring 9% performance gains 
though may not enter into mass production until 2027 at the earliest. 
Longer term, Intel’s next major upgrade will be on 14A which is guided to start risk 
production in 2028 for commercialization in 2029. It is important to note A14 will be the 
first major node Intel provides with its external foundry customers and 0.9 PDK is 
targeted to be shipped in 4Q26. 
For Samsung, at its recent SAFE Forum in 2Q26, the company reaffirmed four 
generations of 2nm roadmap, including SF2 which has been in production since 2025, 
SF2P in 2026, SF2P+ in 2027-28 and SF2X (no firm date). Notably, the previously 
announced derivative technologies, including SF2Z featuring backside power for HPC 
applications and SF2A for automotive applications, were not included in its latest 
roadmap which should be folded as part of its SF2P+ offerings. With a modest delay for 
its 2nm progress, the company also pushed out its SF1.4 mass production to 2029 (vs. 
original 2027). 
On the capacity and yield planning for its advanced nodes, Samsung noted its SF2 yield 
is still at 50-60% levels but confirmed its SF2P+ tooling has been in installation through 
2026 for mass production in 2027 in its Taylor fab. Its capacity expansion in the US 
along with the existing 20k WPM capacity on SF2 in Korea put the company’s capacity 
installed base on the node at 35k WPM by the end of 2027.

### Page 15

Exhibit 28: TSMC's front-end manufacturing transistor density vs. Intel 
TSMC is widening its transistor density advantage against Intel as technology advances

Source: BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
Based on the capacity planning and technology roadmap, we believe TSMC retains the 
leadership through its roadmap and yield execution and scale. Specifically, the 
company’s transistor density has been outperforming by ~30% for its N3/N3E vs. Intel 
3/18A. With similar production timing and targeted applications, we estimate the 
transistor density gap could be even widened to ~50%/~60% for TSMC’s upcoming 
A16/A12 nodes when comparing with Intel 18A-P/14A. 
In addition to delivering superior specs on the power, performance and area, TSMC’s 
execution reliability has become self-reinforcing: superior yield and on-time delivery on 
the leading edge technologies win customer trust. The profitable business opportunity 
then translates the cash flow funds to the next leg of capex cycle required for further 
technology and capacity lead. In contrast to TSMC’s 150-200k+ WPM capacity scale on 
every major node at mature yield, Samsung and Intel running their business at 15-20% 
of the company’s scale at 60-70% yield is not sufficient to meet their own demand nor 
the stringent requirement from the external customers especially as the opportunity cost 
gets higher on the advanced manufacturing. 
On the back-end packaging and testing solutions, TSMC provides its customers with 
higher complexity of the system level integration across advanced logic dies, CPO 
components and HBM through its CoWoS and SoIC technologies for the compute 
density and efficiency optimization. To accommodate larger size package, it is rolling out 
5.5-reticle CoWoS in ’26 with yield reaching 98%+ and will push the technology to 
9.5x/14x reticle limit in ’27/’28 
Longer term, the company is developing SoW for up to 40-reticle packaging and CoPoS 
potentially for late ‘28 / early ’29 mass deployment. The company is also upgrading CPO 
with COUPE solutions from PCB and substrate to interposer for 10x power efficiency 
uplift and 20x latency reduction though the contribution would still be limited based on 
our estimate at least through ’27 before accelerating to 1% of its sales in ’28 with more 
scale up switch adoption. 
While CoPoS mass ramp is toward ’29, the CoWoS upgrade at good yield should keep its 
position vs. Intel’s EMIB-T currently at 80-85% pilot yield seeing risk of losing business 
if it fails to deliver 95%+ mass production yield by mid-’27.

### Page 16

SpaceX could be an anchor customer for
TSMC, but not co-owning capacity/tech
On top of the competition risk from the incumbent players, the headline of Elon Musk’s
aggressiveness to build Terafab targeting long term 1 terawatt equivalent chipset
capacity for AI compute deployment in the space term also triggers the investor
concerns on TSMC’s positioning. We believe the risk is overstated as there could be a
delay in the qualification process of Intel’s 18A for external customers and 14A due to IP

and manufacturing flow issues based on the recent supply chain feedback. In addition,
the equipment vendors also noted Terafab is only evaluating on equipment spec and
potential procurement rather than placing firm orders. With extending equipment
delivery lead time, this could put the company in an unfavorable position to receive tools
for the initial production ramp in ’29 it scheduled.
In the mid-term, Terafab’s applications under Texas’s JETI program earlier this year

outlined ~US$119bn of planned investment across 4 phases from ’26-36, with ~60% of
it on tool procurement. The spending in ’26-28 would be highly skewed toward facility
construction before it starts to pull in US$6-8bn equipment per annum from ‘29-36.
We estimate the company will need to spend US$600mn+ for every 1k WPM expansion
on the advanced nodes it targets in the US. Factoring the investment it targets to spend
in the next decade, Terafab could only grow its capacity installed base by ~10k WPM per

annum at maximum to 96k WPM front-end manufacturing capacity installed by ’36, well
below TSMC’s scale, even if it can successfully deliver the technology roadmap through
its own R&D effort and Intel partnership. This would prevent Terafab’s operation from
the economies of scale as the node migration continues, keeping TSMC as the major
foundry partner for Tesla/SpaceX.
Exhibit 29: Wafer supply/demand analysis on Elon Musk's AI deployment target
With Terafab's near term delay and limited long term scale, TSMC will continue to be critical for Musk's ambitious AI compute deployment plan

Wafer supply/demand analysis on Elon Musk's AI deployment target

Source: BofA Global Research estimates
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH
We also analyze the wafer consumption from SpaceX’s annual orbital AI deployment
target in ‘28/’29 of 10GW/100GW and Musk’s long term 1TW target. Assuming the

compute it plans to deploy in ’28-29 is all powered by the most advanced Nvidia’s
Feynman GPUs delivering 6kW per package, this would drive 1.7mn/16.7mn chipset
demand in ’28/’29 respectively which requires 10k/101k monthly advanced wafer
production. From the supply perspective, as Feynman GPU will be built on TSMC’s A16
node, the chipset demand should be fully accommodated. With limited support from
Terafab, SpaceX in this scenario could become one of the largest customers for TSMC

consuming ~20% of its advanced node capacity in ‘29 (vs. ~1% in ’26 and ~2% in ’28).
Longer term, Musk’s ambitious target to deploy 1 terawatt AI compute power annually
would consume 1mn+ wafers per month on the advanced nodes. To have higher level of
the control on capacity sufficiency, Terafab could consider to partner with TSMC though
the likelihood of TSMC licensing technology or operating a fab with Terafab holding a
meaningful share should be low. Specifically, we would note TSMC’s JV fab record is

limited only with WaferTech, SSMC, VIS, JASM, ESMC and AVSM mostly on mature
nodes and it did not co-own a fab with Apple who contributed ~21% of its revenue from
’15-25 mostly on the advanced processes as it keeps full business control and preserves
customer neutrality.

TSMC’s current valuation remains well supported when viewed through both its 
historical context and its relative positioning vs. its customers. In the past decade, 
TSMC’s forward P/E has averaged ~16x with a range of 10-21x through the cycles.

customers’ more pronounced re-ratings. Based on our analysis of forward P/E spreads, 
TSMC’s major customers usually have a 22% average premium vs. TSMC in the long 
term though the current level is at 45%, suggesting TSMC’s valuation has been lagging.

### Page 18

Specifically, the valuation deviation for TSMC’s HPC customers vs. the company has 
widened with 100-150% valuation premium vs. TSMC at this stage (vs. historical 
average of 53%). We believe this is driven by a surge in valuation for the direct 
beneficiaries from AI data center deployment, including the companies designing GPUs, 
ASICs and networking chipsets. 
Exhibit 34: TSMC's consensus earnings vs. P/E trend 
TSMC's share price strength only driven by earnings upgrade so far

Source: BofA Global Research, Bloomberg 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
It is also noteworthy that, while TSMC’s share performance since 2H23 has been solid, it 
is predominantly driven by the earnings upward revisions rather than multiple expansion. 
The company’s NTM P/E has remained largely stable, only lifted to 18x in the past 2 
years vs. its long-term ~17x levels. The company’s ADR though has been performing 
better as it attracts a broader investor base vs. common share, with its premium still at 
10% (vs. the long term average 8%) and at 20x 2027 consensus P/E. 
With its firmer technology leadership, this should bode well for the company’s earnings 
upgrade cycle and its share should also be re-rated at least 20x NTM P/E (24x P/E for 
ADR factoring the premium), back closer to its customers and the broader ecosystem.

### Page 22

Price objective basis & risk 
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMWF / TSM) 
Our PO for TSMC is NT$3,260 per share (US$620 per ADR), based on 20x 2027E P/E. 
TSMC's valuation has traded in the range of 10-26x PE from 2017. 20x is in the mid to 
high end of the historical range, above the average P/E of 16x. We think a PE above the 
average is justified given TSMC's enhanced industry position and mid-to-high 50% 5-
year CAGR in AI should underpin a rerating

Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) greater-than-expected slowdown in global 
smartphone/consumer electronics demand, (2) Intel's potential insourcing strategy and 
ambitions in foundry service, (3) execution risks on advanced nodes, and (4) higher than 
expected tariffs.

### Page 4

BT substrate supply/demand gap at 11%/14% in 2027/28E on the back of solid memory demand

We introduce our 2026-28E BT substrate TAM forecast, expanding at a 32% 2026-28E 
CAGR to reach US$16.8bn in 2028E (up from US$7.4bn in 2025; Exhibit 1). This growth is 
primarily driven by solid memory demand in general servers (general servers will account 
for 32% of total BT TAM in 2028E, up from 21% in 2025; Exhibit 2), with increasing 
memory capacity/BT content per server. We expect the memory BT substrate TAM to 
outpace the broader BT substrate TAM with a 41% 2026-28E CAGR, accounting for 77% 
of total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (was 56% in 2025). 
On the other hand, we expect the capacity growth rate to decelerate vs. the previous 
upcycle in 2020-22 (6% 2026-28E CAGR vs. 9% 2020-22 CAGR), as key Asian substrate 
suppliers are placing greater focus on their ABF substrate business, and are hence 
scaling back their BT substrate capacity (including Unimicron, which held 8% market 
share in 2025). 
As such, we now expect the overall BT substrate supply/demand gap to reach 
1%/11%/14% in 2H26/2027/2028 (Exhibit 3), while major BT suppliers have started to 
reduce capacity from 2026, potentially leading to a supply shortage in 2H26. We expect 
BT capacity utilization rates (UTR) to trend up amid a supply-constrained environment 
(surpassing 100% from 2027E per GSe), providing a better BT pricing environment going 
forward. The BT substrate supply tightness since the start of the year has directly led to 
a 10% YTD increase in overall BT substrate pricing, and we believe pricing momentum 
for BT substrates will remain robust in 2H26-2028E. 
Accordingly, we forecast overall BT substrate prices will further increase by 
15%-20%/10%-15% in 2027/28E, benefiting BT substrates’ profitability/pricing for 
Taiwanese players (GMs ranged from 15%-27% in 2Q26 and are projected to increase to 
18%-34%/19%-36%+ in 2027/28E; Exhibit 4).

AI; and (2) upcoming launches of new general server CPU platforms, which will drive 
upgrade demand. We expect the BT substrate market to deliver a 32% 2026-28E CAGR, 
reaching US$16.8bn by 2028E (vs. US$7.4bn in 2025). We expect the memory BT

US$4.1bn in 2025), accounting for 77% of total BT demand by 2028E (vs. 56% in 2025). 
In terms of key end applications (Exhibit 6), we expect smartphone BT substrate TAM to 
grow at a 24% 2026-28E CAGR, accounting for 39% of total BT substrate TAM in 2028E 
(down from 51% in 2025; Exhibit 7). We expect general server BT substrate TAM to grow 
at a faster 42% 2026-28E CAGR, accounting for 32% of total BT substrate TAM in 2028E 
(up from 21% in 2025). Meanwhile, we expect AI server BT substrate TAM to see the 
faster growth among all applications at a 73% 2026-28E CAGR, reaching 9% of total BT 
substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025).

Solid growth in general server BT substrate TAM will support mid- to long-term BT 
market growth 
We expect general server BT substrate TAM to deliver a 42% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$5.3bn in 2028E (up from US$1.6bn in 2025; Exhibit 8), which will account for 32% of 
total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 21% in 2025). We expect general server 
memory BT substrate TAM to grow slightly faster at a 43% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$5.0bn in 2028E (up from US$1.4bn in 2025), accounting for 39% of total memory BT 
substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 20% in 2025). This is driven by solid general server 
shipments (we expect general server shipments to register an 8% 2026-28E CAGR; see 
here; Exhibit 9) alongside increasing memory capacity/BT content per general server 
(Exhibit 10). 
Our US analyst, James Schneider, expects Intel/AMD general server CPU shipment to 
grow at a 28%/36% 2026-28E CAGR, reaching a combined 59mn units in 2028E (vs. 
26mn units in 2025). This supports our constructive view that general servers are the key 
driver of the current BT substrate upcycle, given the strong correlation between memory 
BT substrate TAM growth and general server CPU shipment growth.

The strong growth in memory BT substrate TAM is largely driven by memory shipments

### Page 8

Strong AI server shipment growth in 2026-28E will also support BT TAM expansion 
We expect AI server BT substrate TAM to deliver a 73% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$1,476mn in 2028E (up from US$160mn in 2025; Exhibit 14), accounting for 9% of 
total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025). We expect AI server memory BT 
substrate TAM to grow slightly faster at a 75% 2026-28E CAGR and reach US$1,428mn 
in 2028E (up from US$145mn in 2025), accounting for 9% of total memory BT substrate 
TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025). This is driven by solid growth in AI server shipments, 
as we expect AI servers (8-GPU equivalent) and AI server racks (72-GPU equivalent) to 
register 17%/71% 2026-28E CAGRs (see here) with increasing memory capacity per 
server/per server rack. 
We expect GPU/CPU attach rates to move from 2x to 1.1-1.4x over time as agentic AI 
applications become mainstream, implying that each GPU deployment will require more 
server CPUs (see our US analyst Jim Schneider’s Intel initiation). Given that memory BT 
content in AI servers is primarily tied to server CPU count, if the GPU/CPU attach rate 
narrows from 2.0x to 1.1-1.4x, we estimate 1%/1%-2% upside to memory BT substrate 
TAM in 2027/28E. 
For AI server racks (72-GPU equivalent), BT content per rack continues to expand mainly 
due to increasing demand for more memory capacity, resulting in a 50% 2026-28E 
CAGR in BT content per AI server rack (Exhibit 15). A key upgrade is that Nvidia’s VR200 
will support higher DRAM capacity, implying a 100% increase in DRAM memory BT 
substrate content compared to GB200/GB300. We also note Nvidia is releasing a 
standalone Vera CPU rack designed for agentic AI, implying further demand upside for 
AI CPU shipments and memory BT substrates, as each Vera CPU rack incorporates 8x 
more DRAM memory BT substrate content compared to VR200.

grow over coming years which will bode well for BT market

### Page 9

Smartphones remain critical to BT demand with growth going forward tracking 
slower than the overall BT industry 
We expect overall smartphone BT substrate TAM to grow at a 24% 2026-28E CAGR 
(tracking slower than the overall BT industry CAGR of 32%) to reach US$6.5bn in 2028E 
(up from US$3.8bn in 2025), accounting for 39% of total 2028E BT TAM (down from 
51% in 2025). We expect smartphone memory BT substrate TAM to grow at a faster 36% 
2026-28E CAGR to reach US$2.4bn in 2028E (up from US$1.0bn in 2025), accounting 
for 19% of total memory BT TAM in 2028E (down from 24% in 2025). 
As mentioned in our previous update on the BT substrate industry (see here), we believe 
the previous upcycle (in 2020-22) was mainly driven by the proliferation of SiP/AiP 
designs that increased BT substrate content per device, especially for smartphones. 
While smartphones remain critical to overall BT demand, accounting for 51% of total 
2025 BT substrate TAM, we expect their contribution to moderate going forward 
(reaching 39% of 2028E BT TAM). This is driven by softer smartphone shipment volumes 
(we now forecast smartphone shipments at -10%/+2%/+2% YoY in 2026-28E; see here) 
and the fact that BT substrate content per smartphone will be mainly driven by higher 
memory BT substrate pricing (Exhibit 16) rather than other meaningful content value 
gains (memory will account for 46% of smartphone BT ASP in 2028E, up from 32% in 
2025). 
We expect 25%/26%/28% of overall smartphone shipments to be equipped with 
mmWave connectivity in 2026/27/28E (Exhibit 17), resulting in an 18% 2026-28E CAGR 
for smartphone AiP BT TAM to reach US$1,128mn in 2028 (up from US$713mn in 2025). 
For iPhones, mmWave connectivity is only available for models sold in the US 
(representing 35%-40% of total iPhone shipments per GSe). In the Android camp, 
Samsung’s flagship smartphones that support mmWave connectivity are also only for 
the US market.

Automotive BT consumption to steadily increase in coming years 
We expect automotive BT TAM to grow at a 34% 2026-28E CAGR and reach US$777mn 
in 2028E (up from US$317mn in 2025), which will account for 5% of total 2028E BT TAM 
(up from 4% in 2025). This stronger-than-industry growth is mainly driven by rising EV 
penetration going forward (EV penetration rate will increase from 14% in 2025 to 20% in 
2028E per our Japanese auto team; Exhibit 19) and the corresponding increase in 
electronics content per car, which requires more BT substrates. Furthermore, we believe 
rising autonomous driving penetration will result in an increase in electronic 
components/BT consumption per car.

BT market revenue to grow at a 28% 2026-28E CAGR with sustainable memory 
demand

market revenue will account for less than 1% of customers’ overall revenue in 2026-28E 
(down from 2%-3% in the previous upcycle in 2020-22), as customers’ overall growth is 
tracking faster at a 34% 2026-28E CAGR. We believe this lower ratio of BT market

previous upcycle

### Page 14

We have seen BT substrate demand improve since the start of 2026 alongside a firmer 
pricing outlook, and we expect demand growth to consistently outpace supply in 
2026-28E (demand CAGR of 15% vs. supply CAGR of 6%). We believe industry utilization 
rates (UTR) will continue to trend up over the coming years (reaching 100%+ after 
2027E) due to slow supply growth going forward. 
Memory predominantly requires wire bonding chip scale package (WBCSP) technology, 
and this production capacity is not interchangeable with flip-chip chip-scale package 
(FCCSP) technology (where smartphone APs represent the biggest demand driver). As 
such, we believe memory BT substrate supply will remain tight through at least 1H28, as 
new dedicated memory capacity will only come online in 2028. Overall BT pricing has 
already risen by 10% YTD, partially driven by higher material costs (including substrate 
CCL, copper foil, gold, etc., which account for 60% of total BT substrate BOM; Exhibit 
26). Going forward, we believe the slow increase in BT substrate supply, especially 
WBCSP capacity, will be the key price hike driver rather than simply reflecting the 
material cost increase. Furthermore, BT substrates typically account for less than 5% of 
total memory BOM, providing ample room for further BT price hikes amid the rising 
memory pricing trend.

### Page 15

With favorable BT substrate supply/demand conditions, we believe the profitability of 
key BT vendors will see a structural increase in the coming years. Similar to current 
dynamics in the ABF market, the BT substrate industry’s profitability profile has 
navigated three upcycles and three downcycles over the past 20 years (Exhibit 27). 
Overall margin profiles for key BT vendors have begun improving since the start of 2026, 
supported by increasing memory demand amid a supply-constrained environment, 
especially following several years of slow capex investment. We believe this profitability 
enhancement will continue in 2H26-2028, supported by sustained industry demand 
uptrend.

### Page 16

Optical module mSAP is seeing a widening S/D gap, prompting a shift of BT capacity to meet strong 
demand

We introduce our 2026-28E 800G+ optical module mSAP TAM forecast, expanding at an 
87% 2026-28E CAGR to reach US$7.1bn by 2028E (up from US$0.5bn in 2025; Exhibit 
28). This is based on our 800G+ optical module shipment estimates of a 48% 2026-28E 
CAGR (see here). The connectivity PCB within the 800G+ optical module requires mSAP 
technology, where several PCB makers have exposure to and are able to leverage their 
extensive mSAP experience from smartphone substrate-like PCB (SLP) production. 
On the other hand, major PCB and substrate suppliers are placing greater focus on this 
emerging business and pursuing proactive capacity expansion plans, especially ZDT and 
Compeq (the latter aims to ramp optical module mSAP capacity to reach monthly 
revenue of ~NT$3bn by end 2027 vs. less than NT$100mn as of 2Q26; see here). 
Although Unimicron was a front-runner in the 800G+ optical module mSAP business in 
2025 (29% market share in 2025 per GSe), the company is placing greater focus on its 
ABF substrate business, and hence leaving room for emerging peers to capture market 
share through aggressive expansion plans and technology leadership. 
We believe ZDT will become the market share leader in the optical module mSAP 
industry, as we expect the company to expand mSAP capacity by 5x over the next two 
years, positioning it well to gain share in a supply-constrained environment (our industry 
checks indicate mSAP is the most supply-constrained component in the 1.6T optical 
transceiver supply chain). Furthermore, the company currently holds an industry-leading 
yield of 90%+ for its optical module mSAP products. 
Overall, we expect the 800G+ optical module mSAP supply/demand gap to reach 
5%/25%/38% in 2026/27/28E (Exhibit 29). Although major mSAP suppliers continue 
to have proactive expansion plans, this appears insufficient to keep pace with strong 
demand. The 800G+ optical module mSAP tightness since the start of 2026 has directly 
led to a 20%-30% pricing increase YTD, and we believe the pricing outlook for 800G+ 
optical module mSAP will remain solid in 2027/28E.

### Page 23

Investment thesis, PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Zhen Ding Technology 
Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in 
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in 
Shenzhen, China; it has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market compared 
to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% ABF 
substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the most 
from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025-27E CAGR) due to 
the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. We also 
expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to drive 
ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 39% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We see 
further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due to 
our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which 
should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long 
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth 
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+ 
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China 
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.  
Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$1,025 is based on a SOTP valuation methodology, 
including (1) 3.5x blended 2028E P/B for ABF & BT substrate, (2) 20x 2028E P/E for 
high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in line with industry average), and (3) 8x 
2028E P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB (in line with industry downcycle 
average valuation). 
Key downside risks include: (1) weaker-than-expected iPhone demand or slower 
content upgrade, (2) delay in new substrate capacity or slower-than-expected yield rate 
ramp up progress, and (3) fiercer competition in China ABF market.

### Page 24

Investment Thesis - Kinsus
Kinsus is one of the key IC substrates suppliers globally with ~10% ABF/BT substrates
market share in 2024, with ~18% of total 2025 revenue contributed by its contact lens
subsidiary, Pegavision (6941.TW, Not Covered). With our view that the ABF substrate
shortage started from 2Q26 (ABF substrate return to supply demand balance from late
2025), we believe Kinsus’ performance will continue to improve compared with high-end

ABF peers considering the company’s increasing market share in the high-end ABF
substrate market; however, ~36% of the company’s revenue was contributed by BT
substrates in 2025 and 30% of the company’s ABF revenue is from non-LTA customers,
which we believe will continue to enjoy a better pricing outlook. As a result, we believe
Kinsus’ earnings outlook should continue to be strong (~118% 2026-29E CAGR), mainly
driven by better BT substrate and non-LTA ABF substrate pricing outlook, despite the

unfavorable demand outlook before 1H26. We believe Kinsus is an experienced
substrate supplier with a clear capacity expansion plan that can also upgrade its product
portfolio in the long term. We believe we are at the beginning of an industry upcycle,
making us positive on Kinsus’ stock performance over the next 12 months.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Kinsus
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,515 for Kinsus is based on

19.5x 2028E P/E, which is +2.0x STDV higher than Kinsus’ past upcycle average P/E
(14.2x).
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, (3)
Slower/Less-than-expected AI server orders from 2H26.
Investment Thesis - NYPCB
NYPCB manufactures and markets printed circuit boards (PCBs) and integrated circuit
(IC) substrates (including ABF and BT substrates). We are Buy rated on NYPCB

considering the company’s high exposure to BT substrates and non-LTA ABF substrate
(70%+ of total revenue), where we expect GM/OPM to increase significantly in the
coming quarters (from breakeven in OPM in 1H25 to 26/42% in 2026/27E) due to the
favorable substrate pricing outlook. Despite the company’s low exposure to high-end
ABF substrate industry (<20% of total revenue in 2025), we believe the expanding
demand from ASIC AI server and high-end switch IC demand will drive the company’s

high-end ABF revenue exposure to 40%+ by 2027E, suggesting better GM/OPM outlook
in the long term. In 2H25-2H26, we believe NYPCB will continue to benefit from stronger
ABF/BT pricing and should be a key beneficiary of the pricing uptrend considering the
company’s key focus on BT and non-LTA ABF substrate industries. Moreover, in the long
term (after 2026), we expect the ABF industry to return to supply tightness, and NYPCB’s
expanding high-end exposure will lead to a long-term earnings growth outlook (148%

2026-28E earnings CAGR). As a result, we see solid upside potential for NYPCB’s
earnings and stock performance.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - NYPCB
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$2,700 for NYPCB is based
on 21x 2028E P/E, which is +1.5x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E (13.3x).
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)

Slower-than-expected NYPCB new high-end capacity qualification progress.
Investment Thesis - Unimicron Technology
Unimicron is a key supplier of ABF substrates (25% market share in 2025) and BT
substrates and also produces FPC/HDI/PCB products. We are Neutral rated on the
shares given long-term growth opportunity prospects: (1) over 70% of ABF shipments
are covered by long-term agreements (LTA), which limits pricing flexibility and makes

price hike less likely; (2) the company’s execution track record has been weaker than
peers; (3) capacity expansion plan remains slower than peers, constraining its ability to
capture incremental AI demand. The current ABF substrates market is seeing more
favorable supply demand outlook, and Unimicron is likely to see better pricing outlook
to reflect rising material costs in the coming quarters. AI ABF account for 10% of the
company’s total revenue, but the growth is likely to be slower than peers due to the

company continuing to lose market share to peers given its less favourable production
yield and slow capacity expansion. Overall, we are Neutral rated on Unimicron given our
outlook for limited upside potential, barring ASIC AI servers, despite our generally
positive view in the long term on its profitability trajectory.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Unimicron Technology
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,270 for Unimicron is

based on 17.4x 2028E P/E, which is 2.7x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E
(10.3x).
Key upside/downside risks: (1) Slower/faster-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower/faster-than-expected ABF substrates upgrading pace, and (3) Slower/faster
than-expected AI server PCB market share loss progress.
15 October 2026   25

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
ICA/IPO: Gaining traction, but cost still a hurdle. A China CSP outlined optical
form factors from XPO/NPO to interposer optics (IPO) and CPO, including

integration of IPO with interposer copper attach (ICA). TE Connectivity showcased
224G/448G NPO/CPO sockets and ICA sockets with 0.4mm pitch and up to 10,000
pins, offering high-density connectivity, low crosstalk and improved reworkability.
We see rising traction for ICA/IPO, with potential mass production in 2027   (For
more, see our recent CPO report).
Marvell IMC: Complementary to AST2700, not a replacement. Marvell
demonstrated the OCTEON-based infrastructure management controller (IMC)

addresses higher-level Al rack/cluster telemetry and control; ASPEED's AST2700
BMC continues to handle node-level security and remote management. Given
distinct functions and software ecosystems, we do not view IMC as a direct
AST2700 competitor. We also don't see an AST2700 production issue, supporting
an on-track ramp. The key debate is actually market share in TPU/Axion.
Caliptra: Expanding hardware Root of Trust opportunities. The open Caliptra

framework defines silicon-level Root of trust (RoT) for data-center processors and
accelerators, supporting secure boot, device identity and attestation eMemory
(3529.TW) subsidiary PUFsecurity offers PUFrt, providing NeoPUF-derived device,
NeoFuse OTP secure storage and hardware entropy as physical security building
blocks for Caliptra implementations. Broader adoption could support incremental
PUF/security-IP licensing, although monetization depends on qualifications and

customer design wins. (For more, see our quantum security report).
Other: CXL fabric, cooling and compute ramps: The microchannel lid
demonstration underscored the importance of chip-level thermal design as rack

Key Takeaways
ICA/IPO traction: China CSP and TE demonstrations suggest growing adoption,
although cost could be higher
IMC versus BMC: Marvell IMC complements AST2700; we see no production
disruption.
Hardware security: Caliptra strengthens the case for eMemory PUF-based
security IP.
CXL and thermal: CXL supports memory pooling: Cooling and ASIC/CPU ramps

remain in focus. Muse is likely driving storage server demand.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O or Over) - The stock's total return is expected to exceed the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry
team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis over the next 12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E or Equal) - The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or

industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis over the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR) - Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's
industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U or Under) - The stock's total return is expected to be below the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry

team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

四、維持買進理由 
2026 年受到美伊戰爭引發原物料成本提升且戰爭仍未
平息影響全球消費景氣，輔以客戶下單仍顯保守；我們
預估百和 2026 年營收為 161.25 億元 (+2.2% YoY)，其
中傳產中個位數成長 、 網布中~高個位數衰退 ， 下修EPS
為 2.48 元。展望 2027 年，隨著品牌對應庫存調整到較
健康的水位，下單可望回到正常模式。大環境穩定後，
原物料成本可以趨於正常、關稅議題減緩，2027 年可望
優於 2026 年，預估 2027 年 EPS 3.23 元。股價方面，
考量最壞已過 、2027 年可望恢復成長且目前股價低於淨
值，故維持買進評等，目標價 48 元(15X 2027PER)。

## Document 277: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13026023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1). 
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 6.2% YoY and 5.1% YoY excluding gasoline in September 
Total card spending growth per household, based on Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card data (monthly, MoM%, seasonally adjusted (SA)) and 
(monthly, YoY%, non-SA, right-hand side (rhs))

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data. See methodology for category aggregation definitions.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at the share of “more discretionary” retail spending categories that are increasing YoY underscores that growth is well 
balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort… 
Total card spending per household by income tercile and the top 5% 
(three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 8: …though discretionary spending for the top 5% has eased 
some since June 
Discretionary card spending per household by income tercile and the top 
5% (three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.  
Exhibit 9: Necessity spending has gone from a tailwind to a 
headwind across all income cohorts

a narrowing gap

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories 
Share of spending by the top 5% of households by income across select discretionary categories (three-month moving average to September 2026, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).  
Exhibit 13: 8.3% of Bank of America households had a BNPL 
payment in September

The tendency towards greater usage is occurring across income cohorts, though is somewhat more pronounced for lower-
income households (Exhibit 15). However, higher-income households have the largest share of BNPL payments above $500 
(Exhibit 16). Overall, in our view, the increase in the share of BNPL users heading into the season does not appear too 
concerning, though the rate of usage for some BNPL consumers does bear watching.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 323: Indonesia_ Policy mix aligns (2026-10-12)

SHIFT IN NARRATIVE

After a rough first half of the year, the 
narrative around Indonesia’s economy has 
turned relatively constructive . The macro 
outlook is on the mend, and policy coherence 
has strengthened. Recent budget 
developments have reduced the likelihood of an 
outright fiscal slippage. A more disciplined 
fiscal trajectory should also provide room for 
monetary policy to stay focused on its price 
stability mandate rather than on ad dressing 
financial stability risks due to fiscal concerns.

On the markets front, e ven as external triggers 
impacted the rupiah, the one -sided sharp 
depreciation run in the currency in the first half 
of the year has stalled . Debt markets have  
attracted offshore interest, alongside strong 
demand for shorter-tenor SRBI securities, all of 
which have contributed to the overall capital 
flows picture.

Ahead of MSCI’s final take on the domestic 
equity market’s classification in November, 
however, equity markets  find little favour . 
Nonetheless, material improvement s in the 
stock listing s and regulatory requirements 
undertaken by the stock market and financial 
regulators are expected to contribute to the 
medium-term stability of the capital markets.

Key domestic risks that plagued the economy 
in the first half of the year have been alleviated 
to some extent, while a few other monitorables 
remain.

www.dbs.com/insightsdirect/macro groupresc@dbs.com

• Key domestic policy concerns 
have eased. 
• Policy alignment has improved.

allow BI to remain focused on 
price stability.

widen as the trade surplus 
narrows.

however, narrow the BOP 
shortfall.

heavily influenced by global

NEAR-TERM FISCAL WORRIES 
RECEDE

Fiscal fears have moderated in the near 
term. New Finance Minister Suahasil 
Nazara reaffirmed the government’s 
commitment to maintain the annual 
deficit below -3% of GDP threshold. The 
2026 run-rate suggests that the deficit is 
likely to close within the budgeted target.

By September 2026, the fiscal deficit 
stood at 1.2% of GDP , reflecting a 
normalisation in spending and firm 
revenue growth. Total spending rose by 
a still -strong 1 9% yoy , even though the 
pace moderated from 1H, backed by 
disbursements towards the flagship free 
food program, subsidy and 
compensation outlays , as well as 
personnel expenditure.  Realised 
expenditure stood at 69% of the full -
year budget by 9M26.

On the other hand, revenues rose sharply 
by 25 .5% yoy (partly benefiting from 
base effects as readings were negative 
at the same time last year), helped by 
higher tax collections, export duties, and 
non-tax receipts. Revenues have already 
accounted for 74% of the budgeted 
scale by 9M26, suggesting full -year 
target is likely to be met.

Global crude prices are a key risk for the 
fiscal outlook, as prevailing Brent prices 
are above $100bl at the time of writing 
(vs budgeted ICP at $70bl), which will 
feed through higher subsidy and 
compensation outgoes, as authorities 
remain hesitant to raise fuel pump prices.

Significant efficiency measures have, 
however, been put in place  to contain 
spending, including reductions in 
outlays towards flagship schemes , 
which will help contain the extent of 
deterioration in the fiscal math. For 
instance, the flagship MBG program, 
which started with an allocation of IDR 
35trn, was  moderated to IDR 268trn 
(realised spending by Sep26 was IDR 
153trn). Successive stimulus packages

### Page 3

Indonesia: Policy mix aligns October 12, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

have been announced but the scale has 
been contained.

2027 math signals fiscal prudence, soft 
spots warrant attention

The official goalposts for 2027 point to 
a continued preference for fiscal 
prudence rather than fiscal activism.

Spending is on course to surpass the 
symbolic IDR 4000tn level, ris ing nearly 
4% yoy, with outlays divided between 
the centre and regional governments. 
The mix points to steady allocations 
towards subsidies, social transfers , and 
capital expenditure,  with ministry -level 
spending (K/L) set to contract, while non-
K/L spending is budgeted to increase. 
More notably, spending is expected to 
rise on key flagship programs – for 
instance, social protection (up 10%), the 
largest component education (up 14%) , 
which includes the free meals program 
at IDR 240trn.

On the revenue end, state revenues are 
expected to rise by 9% yoy compared to 
the budgeted 2026 APBN/ 7% vs the 
revised 2026 outlook. Compared to 
baseline 2026, tax revenues are 
expected to rise 10.5%, besides higher 
non-tax collections. This math lands the 
2027 budgeted deficit at -2.4% of GDP 
vs revised 2026 at -2.85% of GDP.

Few uncertainties that loom.  Firstly, 
next year’s math rests on an upbeat 
growth projection of 6 .0% (DBSf 5.2%) , 
besides modest depreciation in the 
rupiah and about  a 7% increase in the 
Indonesia crude price assumption (see 
table). Some of these expectations 
might be challenged as the year unfolds. 
Next, state revenues are expected to 
rise to a n upbeat  12.5% of GDP , likely 
riding on a strong growth forecast, 
better tax due diligence , and improved 
administration via the Coretax 
mechanism.

Thirdly, crude prices are a key risk to the 
fiscal math. Given the economy’s 
position as a net oil importer, various 
official commentaries put the sensitivity  
at every $1pb oil worsening the fiscal 
balance by IDR 6trn, after accounting for 
oil-related revenues. This implies that for 
every $10pb increase, the deficit could 
be impacted by ~IDR 60trn, not far from 
MOF’s sensitivity of IDR 68trn (~0.25 -
0.30% of GDP) due to higher subsidy and 
compensation costs  (ICT assumed at 
$75bl vs prevailing Brent at $100bl). 
Spending will need to reprioritised to 
accommodate additional allocation 
towards subsidies and compensation.
